1. Introduction to Genuin Advertising
Genuin is a short-form video experience platform that enables destination brands — publishers, retailers, and media companies — to embed rich video carousels and feeds directly on their websites and apps. These experiences are powered by the Genuin SDK and back-end content infrastructure, providing a native, high-engagement environment for both organic content and advertising.
This guide is the definitive reference for activating, configuring, and managing all ad formats available on the Genuin platform. It covers both in-stream ads (which play inside the video player) and in-feed ads (which appear as cards within the scrollable feed), along with the step-by-step workflows for Genuin's Brand Control Center (BCC), the Aniview ad server, and direct sponsored content creation.
1.1 Understanding Placements: Onsite vs Offsite
Genuin supports two distinct deployment models that determine how inventory is structured and how ads are requested.

1.3 The In-Feed Waterfall Model
When a feed slot is identified as an ad slot, Genuin resolves which ad to show using a sequential waterfall. Higher-priority types are attempted first; the next type is tried only if the higher-priority demand returns no fill.

2. In-Feed Ads: Overview &amp; Architecture
In-feed ads appear as discrete cards within the Genuin video feed — indistinguishable in position from regular content cards, but serving commercial purposes. Unlike in-stream ads (which interrupt an actively playing video), in-feed ads occupy defined slot positions in the scrollable feed.
Genuin's in-feed system supports both direct-sold sponsored content (served via the Genuin backend) and programmatic demand (served via the Aniview ad server using IMA SDK or GPT.js). When a feed slot is resolved as an ad slot, the waterfall described in Section 1.3 determines which ad type is served.
<w:tblPr><w:tblW w:type="dxa" w:w="9360"/><w:tblBorders><w:top w:val="single" w:color="auto" w:sz="4"/><w:left w:val="single" w:color="auto" w:sz="4"/><w:bottom w:val="single" w:color="auto" w:sz="4"/><w:right w:val="single" w:color="auto" w:sz="4"/><w:insideH w:val="single" w:color="auto" w:sz="4"/><w:insideV w:val="single" w:color="auto" w:sz="4"/></w:tblBorders></w:tblPr><w:tblGrid><w:gridCol w:w="9360"/></w:tblGrid><w:tr><w:tc><w:tcPr><w:tcW w:type="dxa" w:w="9360"/><w:tcBorders><w:top w:val="single" w:color="2563EB" w:sz="1"/><w:left w:val="single" w:color="2563EB" w:sz="12"/><w:bottom w:val="single" w:color="2563EB" w:sz="1"/><w:right w:val="single" w:color="2563EB" w:sz="1"/></w:tcBorders><w:shd w:fill="EFF4FF" w:val="clear"/><w:tcMar><w:top w:type="dxa" w:w="120"/><w:left w:type="dxa" w:w="160"/><w:bottom w:type="dxa" w:w="120"/><w:right w:type="dxa" w:w="160"/></w:tcMar></w:tcPr><w:p><w:pPr><w:spacing w:before="0" w:after="0"/></w:pPr><w:r><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Arial" w:cs="Arial" w:eastAsia="Arial" w:hAnsi="Arial"/><w:b/><w:bCs/><w:color w:val="2563EB"/><w:sz w:val="20"/><w:szCs w:val="20"/></w:rPr><w:t xml:space="preserve">ℹ  Phase 1 (current): In-feed ads are supported on Web only. iOS and Android SDK support is on the roadmap.
2.1 How In-Feed Slots Are Identified
The in-feed ad slot engine calculates which positions in the video feed should be occupied by ads, based on two configuration parameters:

The slot engine checks both a swipe-count threshold and a time-elapsed threshold before showing the next ad in the sequence. The stricter of the two applies.

2.2 View Context: Card View vs Full-Screen View
In-feed ads can be configured to appear in one or both viewing contexts:
Card View — Ads appear as thumbnail cards in the scrollable carousel/grid layout.
Full-Screen View — Ads appear when a user expands a video to full-screen. The ad occupies the full viewport.
Both — The default configuration shows ads in both contexts.

Granular breakdown by view context is not available in the current version.
3. In-Feed Ad Types
This section provides full specifications for each of the five in-feed ad types available on the Genuin platform.
3.1 Sponsored Takeover
<w:tblPr><w:tblW w:type="dxa" w:w="9360"/><w:tblBorders><w:top w:val="single" w:color="auto" w:sz="4"/><w:left w:val="single" w:color="auto" w:sz="4"/><w:bottom w:val="single" w:color="auto" w:sz="4"/><w:right w:val="single" w:color="auto" w:sz="4"/><w:insideH w:val="single" w:color="auto" w:sz="4"/><w:insideV w:val="single" w:color="auto" w:sz="4"/></w:tblBorders></w:tblPr><w:tblGrid><w:gridCol w:w="9360"/></w:tblGrid><w:tr><w:tc><w:tcPr><w:tcBorders><w:top w:val="none" w:color="FFFFFF" w:sz="0"/><w:left w:val="single" w:color="7C3AED" w:sz="16"/><w:bottom w:val="none" w:color="FFFFFF" w:sz="0"/><w:right w:val="none" w:color="FFFFFF" w:sz="0"/></w:tcBorders><w:shd w:fill="F5F3FF" w:val="clear"/><w:tcMar><w:top w:type="dxa" w:w="120"/><w:left w:type="dxa" w:w="200"/><w:bottom w:type="dxa" w:w="120"/><w:right w:type="dxa" w:w="200"/></w:tcMar></w:tcPr><w:p><w:pPr><w:spacing w:before="0" w:after="0"/></w:pPr><w:r><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Arial" w:cs="Arial" w:eastAsia="Arial" w:hAnsi="Arial"/><w:b/><w:bCs/><w:color w:val="7C3AED"/><w:sz w:val="18"/><w:szCs w:val="18"/></w:rPr><w:t xml:space="preserve">Direct Sold  |  Genuin Backend  |  Special Slot Logic
Description: A Sponsored Takeover converts the first 5 slots of the video feed into a continuous, brand-sponsored experience. All 5 slots show the sponsor's video content rather than organic feed posts. This is the highest-impact in-feed placement available on Genuin.
The Sponsored Takeover overrides all other in-feed ad types for its occupied slots. It is a direct-sold placement — there is no programmatic path for takeovers. Content is uploaded by the partner brand and served via the Genuin video backend.

3.2 Sponsored Posts
<w:tblPr><w:tblW w:type="dxa" w:w="9360"/><w:tblBorders><w:top w:val="single" w:color="auto" w:sz="4"/><w:left w:val="single" w:color="auto" w:sz="4"/><w:bottom w:val="single" w:color="auto" w:sz="4"/><w:right w:val="single" w:color="auto" w:sz="4"/><w:insideH w:val="single" w:color="auto" w:sz="4"/><w:insideV w:val="single" w:color="auto" w:sz="4"/></w:tblBorders></w:tblPr><w:tblGrid><w:gridCol w:w="9360"/></w:tblGrid><w:tr><w:tc><w:tcPr><w:tcBorders><w:top w:val="none" w:color="FFFFFF" w:sz="0"/><w:left w:val="single" w:color="7C3AED" w:sz="16"/><w:bottom w:val="none" w:color="FFFFFF" w:sz="0"/><w:right w:val="none" w:color="FFFFFF" w:sz="0"/></w:tcBorders><w:shd w:fill="F5F3FF" w:val="clear"/><w:tcMar><w:top w:type="dxa" w:w="120"/><w:left w:type="dxa" w:w="200"/><w:bottom w:type="dxa" w:w="120"/><w:right w:type="dxa" w:w="200"/></w:tcMar></w:tcPr><w:p><w:pPr><w:spacing w:before="0" w:after="0"/></w:pPr><w:r><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Arial" w:cs="Arial" w:eastAsia="Arial" w:hAnsi="Arial"/><w:b/><w:bCs/><w:color w:val="7C3AED"/><w:sz w:val="18"/><w:szCs w:val="18"/></w:rPr><w:t xml:space="preserve">Direct Sold  |  Genuin Backend  |  Frequency-Based Slot Logic
Description: Sponsored Posts are individual in-feed cards containing brand-sponsored video content. Unlike the Takeover (which claims blocks of slots), Sponsored Posts follow the standard slot frequency logic — they appear at the calculated ad slot positions within the feed, interleaved with organic content.
Sponsored Posts are the primary direct-sold in-feed format and are enabled by default in the configuration waterfall. The content partner uploads the sponsored video, which is then distributed by Genuin's backend into the appropriate feed slots.

3.3 Programmatic In-Feed Video Ads

Description: Programmatic in-feed video ads are video creatives served via Google's IMA SDK (Interactive Media Ads SDK) and fulfilled by the Aniview ad server. They appear as video cards within the feed. The creative renders using standard IMA SDK video playback — there are no engagement icons (like/comment/share), and a blue CTA bar replaces the interaction layer.

3.3.2 Key OpenRTB Bid Request Parameters
The following OpenRTB parameters are passed in the bid request from Genuin to Aniview for programmatic in-feed video ads. These are automatically populated by the SDK based on ads_config.json settings and page context.

3.4 Programmatic In-Feed Native Ads

Description: Programmatic in-feed native ads are static card-format creatives served via Google Publisher Tag (GPT.js) and fulfilled by the Aniview ad server. Unlike video ads, native ads use a static layout — a headline, image, brand logo, and description — that blends with the card-based feed aesthetic.

The GPT slot is rendered inside the feed card container and must match the card dimensions of your placement.
3.5 Programmatic In-Feed Display Ads

4. In-Stream Video Ads
The Genuin video carousel player supports in-stream linear video ads that play within the content viewer. These ads run programmatically through the Aniview ad server and are delivered via VAST/VMAP.
4.1 Supported Ad Positions

Figure 4 — In-stream pre-roll ad rendering within the Genuin video player (US Weekly example).
4.3 How In-Stream Ad Serving Works
4.3.1 Prefetch Model
The player uses a non-blocking prefetch model. Ads are requested in advance of the break point, so content continues to play while the ad loads. The ad fires as soon as it is ready.
Pre-roll: ad request sent at content start
Mid-roll: ad request sent ~10–12 seconds before midpoint
Post-roll: ad request sent ~10 seconds before content ends
4.3.2 Server-Side Timeout
Each demand source has a configurable maximum response time (default 2,000 ms). This value is tunable per demand source in BCC. Experiments are recommended to find the ideal timeout — a lower timeout reduces latency but may increase unfilled breaks.
4.3.3 Skippability
The player respects the skipOffset defined in the VAST creative. After the skip offset elapses, users can either tap the skip control or swipe past the ad. Ads beyond 15 seconds must be skippable per IAB guidelines. Shorter ads may be non-skippable.

This is recommended for partner environments where user experience is a priority.
4.3.4 Dynamic Cue Points
Break positions are determined dynamically based on content video length — there are no fixed timestamps. This ensures appropriate ad placement across short-form content of varying durations.
4.4 In-Stream Ad Ops Recommendations
Use 15–30 second creatives for best performance in short-form environments.
Target ad response within 2–6 seconds for programmatic demand to reduce timeouts.
Limit VAST wrapper depth to 1–2 layers to minimise latency.
Configure server-side timeout per demand source — test at 2,000 ms and adjust based on fill rate and latency.
One ad per request — podding is not supported.
Vertical creatives (9:16) are strongly recommended for mobile-first placements.

5. Step-by-Step: Activating Advertising on Genuin
This section is the operational guide for activating each ad type on your Genuin-powered placements. Follow the two-step process: Step 1 — Configure your Monetisation Preset (set the strategic parameters for which ad types to run and how often), then Step 2 — Activate each ad type following the specific workflow for Direct Sponsored Ads, In-Feed Programmatic, and In-Stream Programmatic.
<w:tblPr><w:tblW w:type="dxa" w:w="9360"/><w:tblBorders><w:top w:val="single" w:color="auto" w:sz="4"/><w:left w:val="single" w:color="auto" w:sz="4"/><w:bottom w:val="single" w:color="auto" w:sz="4"/><w:right w:val="single" w:color="auto" w:sz="4"/><w:insideH w:val="single" w:color="auto" w:sz="4"/><w:insideV w:val="single" w:color="auto" w:sz="4"/></w:tblBorders></w:tblPr><w:tblGrid><w:gridCol w:w="9360"/></w:tblGrid><w:tr><w:tc><w:tcPr><w:tcW w:type="dxa" w:w="9360"/><w:tcBorders><w:top w:val="single" w:color="2563EB" w:sz="1"/><w:left w:val="single" w:color="2563EB" w:sz="12"/><w:bottom w:val="single" w:color="2563EB" w:sz="1"/><w:right w:val="single" w:color="2563EB" w:sz="1"/></w:tcBorders><w:shd w:fill="EFF4FF" w:val="clear"/><w:tcMar><w:top w:type="dxa" w:w="120"/><w:left w:type="dxa" w:w="160"/><w:bottom w:type="dxa" w:w="120"/><w:right w:type="dxa" w:w="160"/></w:tcMar></w:tcPr><w:p><w:pPr><w:spacing w:before="0" w:after="0"/></w:pPr><w:r><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Arial" w:cs="Arial" w:eastAsia="Arial" w:hAnsi="Arial"/><w:b/><w:bCs/><w:color w:val="2563EB"/><w:sz w:val="20"/><w:szCs w:val="20"/></w:rPr><w:t xml:space="preserve">ℹ  Presets are the control layer — they define what can show and when. The activation workflows below then connect each ad type to the content or demand sources that fill those slots.
5.1 Step 1 — Configure Your Monetisation Preset
A Monetisation Preset is the strategic control layer for all ad settings. It determines which ad types are active, their priority in the waterfall, and the frequency and behaviour parameters. Configuring your preset is the first step because it defines the envelope within which all ad types operate.
5.1.1 System Default Presets
Genuin provides four read-only system presets as starting points. Choose the one that best matches your initial monetisation strategy, then copy and customise as needed.

In the preset editor, locate the 'Ad Types &amp; Priority' section. Enable or disable ad types and drag to set their priority order. The type at the top of the list is attempted first for every ad slot.

Click 'Save Preset'. Then use the 'Active Placements' dropdown on the preset card to assign it to one or more placements. A placement can only be assigned to one preset at a time.
Each placement can only be assigned to one active preset at a time. If you attempt to assign a placement already assigned to another preset, BCC will display a conflict warning and ask whether you want to reassign.
5.1.3 In-Feed Configuration Settings
Inside the preset editor, configure the In-Feed section settings for any placement that runs in-feed ads.

RACI: R = Responsible   A = Accountable   C = Consulted   I = Informed
5.2 Step 2 — Activate Ads
With your preset configured, follow the specific activation workflows below for each ad category you want to run. Each workflow is independent — you can activate any combination of Direct Sponsored Ads, In-Feed Programmatic, and In-Stream Programmatic based on your commercial model.
5.2.1 Activate In-Feed Sponsored Ads (Takeovers &amp; Sponsored Posts)
Sponsored Takeovers and Sponsored Posts are direct-sold placements — they bypass the programmatic waterfall and are served directly by the Genuin backend. Both formats are managed end-to-end through the Sponsorship tab in BCC. The workflow below is based on the Sprint 39–41 Figma designs and reflects the intended launch interface.
Creating a Sponsored Post

Fill in the right-hand panel across three sections — Sponsor: enter the Brand Handle (e.g., @walmart); Sponsored Post: set Your Placement (All Placements &amp; Embeds or specific), Environment (Global or platform-specific), Position (toggle 'Pin sponsored posts as a first position'), and Impression Tracking (GTM ID and Pixel URL, both optional); Earnings: set Video View CPM, Total Sponsorship Budget, Sponsorship Period (start and end dates), Pacing (ASAP or Evenly), Payment Type (Genuin Payout), and Billing Cycle (Net 30).

RACI: R = Responsible   A = Accountable   C = Consulted   I = Informed
5.2.2 Activate In-Feed Programmatic Ads
Programmatic in-feed video, native, and display ads are fulfilled via the Aniview ad server. This workflow walks through setting up Aniview channels, configuring ad tag URLs, linking them to your Genuin placements via ads_config.json, and configuring the fallback content that serves when no programmatic demand fills a slot.

Configuring Fallback Content
When no programmatic demand fills an in-feed slot — because all demand sources return empty (no bid, timeout, or floor not met) — the House / Fallback layer activates automatically. Fallback content is always-on and cannot be disabled. Understanding how to configure it ensures that unfilled slots never show blank cards.

RACI: R = Responsible   A = Accountable   C = Consulted   I = Informed
5.2.3 Activate In-Stream Programmatic Ads
In-stream ads play inside the Genuin video player at pre-roll, mid-roll, and/or post-roll positions. They are fulfilled programmatically via the Aniview ad server using VAST/VMAP. Activation involves enabling in-stream in your preset (already covered in Step 1) and connecting an Aniview in-stream channel.
Aniview Channel Setup for In-Stream

RACI: R = Responsible   A = Accountable   C = Consulted   I = Informed
6. Measurement &amp; Reporting
6.1 Impression Counting
An ad impression is counted when the ad is successfully loaded and displayed — it is based on ad load, not on duration watched or user interaction. This aligns with standard IAB impression measurement definitions.

6.2 Reporting Availability
Ad impression reports are available via:
Aniview Dashboard: Granular programmatic reporting — impressions, fill rate, CPM, revenue, broken down by Channel (placement).
Genuin BCC Analytics: Aggregate ad activity for direct-sold (Sponsored Posts, Takeovers). Impression totals, engagement rates, and revenue summaries.

Separate reporting by ad placement type (in-stream vs in-feed) or by view context (card view vs full-screen) is not yet available in the current reporting version.
6.3 Ad Quality &amp; Brand Safety
Programmatic demand is subject to Aniview's brand safety filters. Configure keyword blocklists and category exclusions in your Aniview account settings.
Vertical-only creatives (9:16) can be enforced at the Aniview channel level to maintain feed aesthetics — this is strongly recommended.
Skippable-only demand can be enforced at the request level through Aniview configuration.
Creatives can be reviewed in the Aniview creative library. Report brand safety violations to your Aniview account manager.

7.3 Technical Best Practices
Limit VAST/VMAP wrapper depth to 1–2 layers to reduce latency and timeout rates.
Configure demand source timeouts at 2,000 ms initially and optimise based on fill rate data.
Use 9:16 vertical creatives for all feed placements — this provides the best UX on mobile.
Test frequency settings (start slot + repeat) before going to full traffic — start conservative and ramp up.
For direct sponsored content, always include #ad or #sponsored in the description for compliance.
Monitor back-to-back ad warnings in BCC — when two consecutive ad slots appear, the feed visualiser displays an amber warning indicator.
Ensure GPT.js is loaded before the Genuin SDK initialises on pages serving native/display in-feed ads.

Appendix A: ads_config.json Full Reference
The ads_config.json file is the runtime configuration file read by the Genuin SDK. It is maintained by the Genuin implementation team and updated via BCC. The fields below describe all configurable parameters for in-feed video ads on Web.
